do not rely on Sobolev embeddings and will carry over conveniently to the present context. We start by stating a basic consequence of the global Lipschitz bounds on
f
,
g
and
h
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Pick
k=0
and suppose that
(Hf-Lip)
,
(HSt)
,
(HCor)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied. Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
v\in L^{2}

we have the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|f(\Phi+v)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}&\leq&K[1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}],\\[5.69046pt]
\|g(\Phi+v)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}&\leq&K[1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}],\\[5.69046pt]
\|h(\Phi+v)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}&\leq&K[1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}],\\[5.69046pt]
\end{array}

(4.24)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in L^{2}

we have the estimates

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|f(\Phi+v_{A})-f(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}&\leq&K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}&\leq&K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|h(\Phi+v_{A})-h(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}&\leq&K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{array}

(4.25)

Proof.

This follows immediately from the fact that
f
,
g
and
h
are all globally Lipschitz, that
\Phi(x)
approaches its spatial limits at an exponential rate as
x\to\pm\infty
, and that
\mathbb{T}^{d-1}
is bounded.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

Pick
k=0
and suppose that
(Hf-Lip)
,
(HSt)
,
(HCor)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied. Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
v\in L^{2}

we have the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g(\Phi+v)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};L^{2})}&\leq&K[1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}],\end{array}

(4.26)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in L^{2}

we have the estimates

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};L^{2})}&\leq&K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{array}

(4.27)

Proof.

The assertion follows from Lemmas
4.5
and
4.6
.
∎

We proceed by considering the scalar cut-off functions

\chi_{h}
and
\chi_{l}
defined
in (
A.5
). In addition, we provide

L^{2}_{Q}
-estimates for the
auxiliary function

\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(u,\gamma)=\chi_{l}(u,\gamma)\chi_{h}(u,\gamma)Qg(u)^{\top}T_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw},

(4.28)

which turn out to be highly convenient for our analysis of
b
and
\mathcal{K}_{C}
below.

Lemma 4.8
.

Pick
k=0
and suppose that
(HSt)
,
(Hq)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
v\in L^{2}
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}

we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}|\chi_{h}(\Phi+v,\gamma)|+|\chi_{l}(\Phi+v,\gamma)|+\|\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}&\leq&K.\end{array}

(4.29)

In addition, for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in L^{2}

and any pair
\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\in\mathbb{R}
,
we have the estimates

\begin{array}[]{lcl}|\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})|&\leq&K\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}+|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big],\\[5.69046pt]
|\chi_{l}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\chi_{l}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})|&\leq&K\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}+(1+\|v_{A}\|_{L^{2}})|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big],\end{array}

(4.30)

while the expression

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{AB}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}&=&\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})\end{array}

(4.31)

satisfies the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{AB}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}&\leq&K\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}+(1+\|v_{A}\|_{L^{2}})|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big].\end{array}

(4.32)

Proof.

The results for
\chi_{h}
and
\chi_{l}

follow directly from their definitions in (
A.5
), as in
[
30
, App. A]
. Turning to
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}
, we note
that for any
z\in L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m\times n})

and any
\psi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})

we have

\|Qz\psi\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}^{2}=\langle Qz\psi,z\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m})}\leq\|q\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m\times m})}\|z\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m\times n})}\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{2},

(4.33)

which upon taking
z=g(\Phi+v)^{\top}
and
\psi=T_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}
shows that

\|\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}\leq K\|g(\Phi+v)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}

(4.34)

for any
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
.
We now observe
that whenever
\chi_{h}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\neq 0
holds, the function

\tilde{v}=\Phi+v-T_{\gamma}\Phi_{\rm ref}

(4.35)

necessarily satisfies
\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{2}}\leq 3+\|\Phi_{0}-\Phi_{\rm ref}\|_{L^{2}}
,
which leads to the uniform a priori bound

\|g(\Phi+v)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}=\|g(T_{\gamma}\Phi_{\rm ref}+\tilde{v})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}\leq C^{\prime}

(4.36)

for some
C^{\prime}>0

on account of the global Lipschitz smoothness of
g
and the fact that the quantity
\|g(T_{\gamma}\Phi_{\rm ref})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}
is finite (and independent of
\gamma
).

Turning to the Lipschitz bound (
4.32
) in
L^{2}_{Q}
,
we first compute

\displaystyle\Delta_{AB}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}

\displaystyle=\big[\chi_{l}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\chi_{l}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})\big]Q\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})g(\Phi+v_{A})^{\top}T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}

(4.37)

\displaystyle+\chi_{l}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})Q(\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B}))g(\Phi+v_{A})^{\top}T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}

\displaystyle+\chi_{l}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})Q\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})\big(g(\Phi+v_{A})^{\top}-g(\Phi+v_{B})^{\top}\big)T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}

\displaystyle+\chi_{l}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})Q\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})g(\Phi+v_{B})^{\top}\big(T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}-T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}\big).

Assuming without loss that
\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})\neq 0
(reverse
A
and
B
otherwise),
the result now follows by applying (
4.30
) and the observations above, noting that
\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime}
is uniformly bounded.
∎

We are now ready to consider the function

\mathcal{K}_{C}
defined in (
A.19
),
which can be written as

\mathcal{K}_{C}(u,\gamma)=-\chi_{h}(u,\gamma)g(u)\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(u,\gamma),

(4.38)

together with the function
b
defined in (
A.11
).
Note that the computations in

[
30
, App. A]
provide the
convenient relationship

\|{b}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|^{2}_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};\mathbb{R})}=\chi_{h}(\Phi+v,\gamma)^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}^{2}.

(4.39)

Corollary 4.9
.

Pick
k=0
and suppose that
(HSt)
,
(Hq)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
v\in L^{2}
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}

we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}}+\|b(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};\mathbb{R})}&\leq&K.\end{array}

(4.40)

In addition, for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in L^{2}

and any pair
\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\in\mathbb{R}
,
the expressions

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{AB}\mathcal{K}_{C}&=&\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B}),\\[5.69046pt]
\Delta_{AB}b&=&b(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-b(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})\end{array}

(4.41)

satisfy the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{AB}\mathcal{K}_{C}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq&K\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}+(1+\|v_{A}\|_{L^{2}})|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big],\\[5.69046pt]
\|\Delta_{AB}b\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};\mathbb{R})}&\leq&K\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}+\big(1+\|v_{A}\|_{L^{2}}\big)|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big].\end{array}

(4.42)

Proof.

The estimate (
4.40
) follows directly from Lemma
4.8
,
the identities (
4.38
)–(
4.39
), and from the general inequality

\|\mathcal{K}_{C}(u,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\chi_{h}(u,\gamma)g(u)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};L^{2})}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(u,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}.

(4.43)

The computations in
[
30
, App. A]

yield

\displaystyle\hskip-1.42271pt\|\Delta_{AB}b\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};\mathbb{R})}^{2}=\|\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}^{2},

(4.44)

which leads to the Lipschitz bound for
\Delta_{AB}b
in
L^{2}
after appealing to Lemma
4.8
. The estimate for
\Delta_{AB}\mathcal{K}_{C}
follows analogously.
∎

We now consider the function

\kappa_{\sigma}(u,\gamma)=1+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\|b(u,\gamma)\|^{2}_{HS(\mathcal{W}_{Q};\mathbb{R})},

(4.45)

together with the associated quantities
\nu_{\sigma}^{(\vartheta)}

defined in (
A.13
).
These are the final ingredient required towards analysing the function
a_{\sigma}
that describes the deterministic part of the evolution of the phase.

Lemma 4.10
.

Pick
k=0
together with
\vartheta\in\{-1,-1/2,1\}
and suppose that
(HSt)
,
(Hq)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
\sigma\geq 0
, any
v\in L^{2}
and any
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}

we have the bound

|\nu_{\sigma}^{(\vartheta)}(\Phi+v,\gamma)|\leq\sigma^{2}K,

(4.46)

In addition, for any
\sigma\geq 0
, any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in L^{2}
,
and any pair
\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\in\mathbb{R}
,
we have the estimate

|\nu_{\sigma}^{(\vartheta)}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\nu_{\sigma}^{(\vartheta)}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})|\leq\sigma^{2}K\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}+\big(1+\|v_{A}\|_{L^{2}}\big)|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big].

(4.47)

Proof.

Using Corollary
4.9
,
the bounds can be established as in the proof of

[
27
, Lem. 3.9]
.
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

Pick
k=0
and suppose that
(Hf-Lip)
,
(HSt)
,
(HCor)
,
(Hq)
and
(HPar)
all hold. Then there exists a constant
K_{a}>0
, which does not depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, such that for any
v\in L^{2}
, any
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, and any
0\leq\sigma\leq 1
, we have

|a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v,\gamma;c)|\leq K_{a}(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}).

(4.48)

In addition, for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in L^{2}
, any
\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\in\mathbb{R}
,
and any
0\leq\sigma\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle|a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A}

\displaystyle;c)-a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B};c)|\leq

(4.49)

\displaystyle K_{a}\big[1+\|v_{A}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big]\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}+|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big].

Proof.

Recalling the definition (
A.22
)
and introducing the functions

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{I}(u,\gamma)

\displaystyle=\langle f(u),T_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sigma^{2}\langle h(u),T_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}},

(4.50)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{II}(u,\gamma;c)

\displaystyle=c\langle u,T_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sigma^{2}\langle\mathcal{K}_{C}(u,\gamma;c),T_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}},

(4.51)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{III}(u,\gamma)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\sigma}(u,\gamma)\langle u,T_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}},

(4.52)

together with the shorthand notation

\mathcal{E}(u,\gamma;c)=\mathcal{E}_{I}(u,\gamma)-\mathcal{E}_{II}(u,\gamma;c)+\mathcal{E}_{III}(u,\gamma),

(4.53)

we see that

a_{\sigma}(u,\gamma;c)=-\chi_{l}(u,\gamma)\mathcal{E}(u,\gamma;c).

(4.54)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle|a_{\sigma}(u_{A},\gamma_{A};c)-a_{\sigma}(u_{B},\gamma_{B};c)|\leq|\chi_{l}(u_{A}

\displaystyle,\gamma_{A})-\chi_{l}(u_{B},\gamma_{B})\|\mathcal{E}(u_{A},\gamma_{A};c)|

(4.55)

\displaystyle+|\chi_{l}(u_{B},\gamma_{B})\|\mathcal{E}(u_{A},\gamma_{A};c)-\mathcal{E}(u_{B},\gamma_{B};c)|.

Note that (HPar) implies

|c|+\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{1},

(4.56)

which using Lemmas
4.6
and
4.10
, Corollary
4.9
, and the fact that
\psi_{\rm tw}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
yields

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}(\Phi+v,\gamma;c)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}).

(4.57)

Together with the uniform bound
(
4.29
) for
\chi_{l}
,
we hence obtain (
4.48
) as well as an estimate for the first term of (
4.55
) that can be absorbed in (
4.49
).

We continue by only focusing on the term
\mathcal{E}_{III}
, remarking that
\mathcal{E}_{I}
and
\mathcal{E}_{II}
can be handled in a similar fashion.
Note that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{III}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A})-\mathcal{E}_{III}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B})|\leq

(4.58)

\displaystyle|\kappa_{\sigma}(u_{A},\gamma_{A})-\kappa_{\sigma}(u_{B},\gamma_{B})\|\langle u_{A},T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle+|\kappa_{\sigma}(u_{B},\gamma_{B})\|\langle u_{A},T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\langle u_{B},T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}|,

where
|\kappa_{\sigma}(u,\gamma)|\leq C_{3}

on account of (
4.46
).
Appealing to (
4.47
), we arrive at a bound for the first term of (
4.58
) that can be absorbed in (
4.49
).

Since in fact
\psi_{\rm tw}\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})
holds,
we may use

T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}-T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}=\int_{0}^{\gamma_{B}-\gamma_{A}}T_{\gamma_{A}+s}\psi^{\prime}_{\rm tw}\mathrm{d}s

(4.59)

to conclude

\|T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}-T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\|\psi_{\rm tw}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(4.60)

In order to find an estimate for the second part of (
4.58
),
it suffices to compute

\displaystyle|\langle u_{A},T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\langle u_{B},T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq|\langle u_{A}-u_{B},T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}|+|\langle u_{A},T_{\gamma_{A}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}-T_{\gamma_{B}}\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}}|

(4.61)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\|u_{A}-u_{B}\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\rm tw}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|u_{A}\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\rm tw}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})}|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|,

completing the proof.
∎

4.3
Stochastic terms

We here collect the estimates that we will need for the stochastic terms in our evolution systems. As a preparation, we provide bounds for
g
in
H^{1}
,
H^{k},
and
H^{k+1}
, respectively, noting that the first and the last will be required in §
4.4
below.

Lemma 4.12
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied. Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that for any
v\in H^{1}

we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g(\Phi+v)\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}),\end{array}

(4.62)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{k}
we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n\times m})}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}.\end{array}

(4.63)

Proof.

These bounds follow directly by
noting that
Dg
is uniformly bounded and
by inspecting the pointwise estimate (
4.2
).
∎

Corollary 4.13
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied. Then for any
v\in H^{k}

we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g(\Phi+v)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k})}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1}),\end{array}

(4.64)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{k}
we have the estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k})}&\leq&K\big(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}\big)\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}.\end{array}

(4.65)

Proof.

Using the bound (
4.9
),
these estimates follow by combining
Lemmas
4.1
and
4.5
.
∎

Corollary 4.14
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied. Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that for any
v\in H^{k+1}

we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g(\Phi+v)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k+1})}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1})(1+\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}),\end{array}

(4.66)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{k+1}
the expression

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{AB}g&=&g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\end{array}

(4.67)

satisfies the estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{AB}g\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k+1})}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad\qquad\times(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k+1}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}}.\end{array}

(4.68)

Proof.

Using the bound (
4.10
), these estimates follow by combining
Lemmas
4.2
and
4.5
.
∎

We now turn to the functions
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}

that act on
\xi\in L^{2}_{Q}
as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{S}(v;\Phi)[\xi]&=&g(\Phi+v)[\xi]+\partial_{x}(\Phi+v)b(\Phi+v,0)[\xi],\\[5.69046pt]
\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(v)[\xi]&=&\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,0)^{-1/2}\mathcal{S}(v;\Phi_{\sigma})[\xi].\end{array}

(4.69)

Corollary 4.15
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied. Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that for any
v\in H^{k+1}

and any sufficiently small
\sigma\geq 0

we have the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{S}(v;\Phi)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k})}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1}+\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}),\\[5.69046pt]
\|\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(v)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k})}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1}+\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}).\end{array}

(4.70)

Proof.

These estimates follow readily from
(
4.64
), the inequality in (
4.46
) for
\vartheta=-1/2
,
and the bounds for
b
in Corollary
4.9
.
∎

4.4
Deterministic terms

Proceeding with the terms in the deterministic part of our evolution equations, our ultimate goal is to obtain a bound for the nonlinearity
\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}
that will feature in our stability arguments. One of the main tasks is to track the dependence on the
H^{k+1}
-norm of our perturbation
v(t)
. Indeed, in the sequel we will only have integrated control over this norm, as opposed to the pointwise control that we will have over the
H^{k}
-norm. We start by providing basic bounds for the nonlinearity
f
and correction term
h
.

Corollary 4.16
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(Hf-Lip)
,
(HCor)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied. Then there exists a constant
K>0
, which does not depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that for any
v\in H^{k}

we have the bound

\|f(\Phi+v)\|_{H^{k}}+\|h(\Phi+v)\|_{H^{k}}\leq K(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1}),

(4.71)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{k}
we have the estimate

\|f(\Phi+v_{A})-f(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{k}}\leq K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}},

(4.72)

which also holds for
h
.
Finally, for any
v\in H^{k}
we have the quadratic bound

\|f(\Phi+v)-f(\Phi)-Df(\Phi)[v]\|_{H^{k}}\leq K(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1})\|v\|^{2}_{H^{k}}.

(4.73)

Proof.

These estimates follow from
Lemmas
4.1
and
4.3

together with the bound (
4.9
).
∎

We proceed by studying the term
\mathcal{K}_{C}
, which we must understand in
H^{k+1}
. For any multi-index
\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{\geq 0}
with
|\beta|=1
, we will use the estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\partial^{\beta}\mathcal{K}_{C}(u,\gamma)\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&\|\chi_{h}(u,\gamma)\partial^{\beta}g(U)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k})}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(u,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\|\chi_{h}(u,\gamma)g(U)\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k})}\|\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(u,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}},\end{array}

(4.74)

together with its natural analogue for

\partial^{\beta}[\mathcal{K}_{C}(u_{A},\gamma)-\mathcal{K}_{C}(u_{B},\gamma)]
. We start by considering the
term
\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{K}_{C}
, for which it suffices to understand
g
in
H^{1}
.

Lemma 4.17
.

Pick
k>d/2
together with a multi-index
\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{\geq 0}
with
|\beta|=1

and suppose that
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
,
which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
v\in H^{1}
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}

we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}),\end{array}

(4.75)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{k}

and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
the expression

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{AB}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}&=&\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma)\end{array}

(4.76)

satisfies the estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\partial^{\beta}\Delta_{AB}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}\end{array}

(4.77)

Proof.

Note first
that for any
z\in H^{1}(\mathcal{D},\mathbb{R}^{m\times n})
and any
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}

we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\partial^{\beta}QzT_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}^{2}&=&\|Q\partial^{\beta}[zT_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}]\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}^{2}\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\|q\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m\times m})}\|\partial_{\xi}[zT_{\gamma}\psi_{\rm tw}]\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m})}^{2}\\[5.69046pt]
&\leq&\|q\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m\times m})}\|z\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{m\times n})}\big[\|\psi_{\rm tw}\|_{\infty}+\|\psi_{\rm tw}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\big]^{2}.\end{array}

(4.78)

The bound (
4.75
) hence follows
directly from (
4.62
).
Turning to the estimate
(
4.77
), we recall the identity (
4.37
) and set
\gamma=\gamma_{A}=\gamma_{B}
.
The stated bound can now be obtained by combining
Lemmas
4.8
and
4.12
.
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

Pick
k>d/2

and suppose that
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
,
which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true. For any
v\in H^{k+1}
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}

we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{H^{k+1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}})(1+\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}),\end{array}

(4.79)

while for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{k+1}

and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
the expression

\Delta_{AB}\mathcal{K}_{C}=\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)-\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma)

(4.80)

satisfies the estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{AB}\mathcal{K}_{C}\|_{H^{k+1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad\qquad\times(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k+1}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}}.\end{array}

(4.81)

Proof.

The bound (
4.79
)
follows from the decomposition (
4.74
), using
(
4.29
),
(
4.64
), (
4.66
),
and (
4.75
).
Turning to the Lipschitz estimate in
H^{k}
, we
pick a multi-index
\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}_{\geq 0}
and consider the splitting

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\partial^{\beta}\Delta_{AB}\mathcal{K}_{C}&=&\mathcal{E}_{I}+\mathcal{E}_{II},\end{array}

(4.82)

in which we have defined

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{E}_{I}&=&(\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)-\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma))[\partial^{\beta}g(\Phi+v_{A})]\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B})[\partial^{\beta}g(\Phi+v_{A})-\partial^{\beta}g(\Phi+v_{B})]\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B})\partial^{\beta}g(\Phi+v_{B})\big[\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)-\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma)\big],\\[5.69046pt]
\end{array}

(4.83)

together with

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{E}_{II}&=&(\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)-\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma))[g(\Phi+v_{A})]\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B})[g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})]\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\chi_{h}(\Phi+v_{B})g(\Phi+v_{B})[\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)-\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma)].\\[5.69046pt]
\end{array}

(4.84)

Using decompositions analogous to
(
4.74
),
the bounds in Lemmas
4.8

and
4.17
,
and Corollaries
4.13
and

4.14
, lead to expressions that can all be absorbed in the stated estimate. Indeed, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{E}_{I}\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}}+\|v_{B}\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}})(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k+1}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k+1}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}},\end{array}

(4.85)

together with

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{E}_{II}\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}}+\|v_{B}\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}})(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{A}\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}}+\|v_{B}\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}}))(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{1}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1})(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}},\end{array}

(4.86)

which proves the assertion.
∎

Upon introducing the expressions

\Xi_{I}(u,c)=f(u)+c\partial_{x}u,\qquad\qquad\Xi_{II;\sigma}(u,\gamma)=\sigma^{2}[h(u)+\partial_{x}\mathcal{K}_{C}(u,\gamma)],

(4.87)

we point out that the function
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}

defined in (
A.23
)
can be written in the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(u,\gamma;c)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\sigma}(u,\gamma)^{-1}\big[\Xi_{I}(u,c)+\Xi_{II;\sigma}(u,\gamma)\big].

(4.88)

The estimates above can be used to formulate convenient
bounds for
\Xi_{I}
and
\Xi_{II;\sigma}
.

Corollary 4.19
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(Hf-Lip)
,
(HCor)
,
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
,
which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true. For any
v\in H^{k+1}
, any
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, and any
\sigma\geq 0
,
we have the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\Xi_{I}(\Phi+v,c),\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}),\\[5.69046pt]
\langle\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi+v,\gamma),\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&K\sigma^{2}(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}}),\end{array}

(4.89)

together with

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Xi_{I}(\Phi+v,c)\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1}+\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}\big),\\[5.69046pt]
\|\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi+v,\gamma)\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K\sigma^{2}(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1})(1+\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}).\end{array}

(4.90)

In addition, for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{k+1}
,
any
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
and any
\sigma\geq 0
,
the expressions

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{AB}\Xi_{I}&=&\Xi_{I}(\Phi+v_{A},c)-\Xi_{I}(\Phi+v_{B},c),\\[5.69046pt]
\Delta_{AB}\Xi_{II;\sigma}&=&\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma)-\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma),\end{array}

(4.91)

satisfy the estimates

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\Delta_{AB}\Xi_{I},\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\langle\Delta_{AB}\Xi_{II;\sigma},\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&\sigma^{2}K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\end{array}

(4.92)

together with

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{AB}\Xi_{I}\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|^{k}_{H^{k}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}+K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|\Delta_{AB}\Xi_{II;\sigma}\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad\qquad\times(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k+1}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}}^{k}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}^{k})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k+1}}.\end{array}

(4.93)

Proof.

Inspecting the
definitions (
4.87
),
the
L^{2}
-bounds follow from
Lemma
4.6
and Corollary
4.9
after transferring the spatial derivatives onto
\psi_{\rm tw}
,
while the
H^{k}
-bounds follow from
Corollary
4.16

and Lemma
4.18
.
∎

We are now ready to consider the
nonlinearity
\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}

defined in (
A.28
). It is exactly at this point where we need the pair
(\Phi,c)=(\Phi_{\sigma},c_{\sigma})
to be the instantaneous stochastic waves, which requires
\sigma\geq 0
to be sufficiently small.
In particular,
for any
v\in H^{k+1}

we will use the representation

\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(v)=\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v)-\chi_{l}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,\gamma)\langle\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v),\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}[\partial_{x}\Phi_{\sigma}+\partial_{x}v],

(4.94)

which involves the intermediate function

\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v)=\Phi_{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,0;c_{\sigma})-c_{0}\partial_{x}v-Df(\Phi_{0})v;

(4.95)

see (
A.29
) and (
A.30
).
By construction, we have
\Phi_{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma};0;c_{\sigma})=0
,
which allows us to write

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v)&=&\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,0;c_{\sigma})-\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma},0;c_{\sigma})-c_{0}\partial_{x}v-Df(\Phi_{0})v\\[5.69046pt]
&=&f(\Phi_{\sigma}+v)-f(\Phi_{\sigma})-Df(\Phi_{\sigma})v+\big(Df(\Phi_{\sigma})-Df(\Phi_{0})\big)v+(c_{\sigma}-c_{0})\partial_{x}v\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma},0)^{-1}\big(\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,0)-\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma},0)\big)\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,(\nu^{(-1)}_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,0)-\nu^{(-1)}_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}))\big[\Xi_{I}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,c_{\sigma})+\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,0)\big]\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\nu^{(-1)}_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma},0)\big(\Xi_{I}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v,c_{\sigma})-\Xi_{I}(\Phi_{\sigma},c_{\sigma})\big),\end{array}

(4.96)

recalling the function
\nu^{(-1)}_{\sigma}(u,\gamma)=\kappa_{\sigma}^{-1}(u,\gamma)-1
.

Corollary 4.20
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(Hf-Lip)
,
(HCor)
,
(Hq)
and
(HSt)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0

so that for all
sufficiently small

\sigma\geq 0

and all
v\in H^{k+1}

we have the estimates

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v),\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&K\|v\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{H^{k}}+\sigma^{2}K\|v\|_{L^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v)\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}})\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}K\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+2}\big).\end{array}

(4.97)

Proof.

The
L^{2}
-inner product bound follows from
Lemma
4.10
and
Corollary
4.19
,
together with the estimate
\|v^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|v\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{H^{k}}
.
The
H^{k}
-bounds follow in a similar fashion,
using (
4.73
) to obtain the intermediate estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v)\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|^{k+1}_{H^{k}})\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}K\|v\|_{H^{k}}\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+1})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\sigma^{2}K\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+2}\big),\end{array}

(4.98)

which can be absorbed in the stated bound.
∎

Corollary 4.21
.

Pick
k>d/2
and suppose that
(Hf-Lip)
,
(HCor)
,
(Hq)
and
(HSt)
are satisfied.
Then there exists a constant
K>0

so that for all
sufficiently small

\sigma\geq 0

and all
v\in H^{k+1}

we have the estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(v)\|_{H^{k}}&\leq&K\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{2}(1+\|v\|^{k}_{H^{k}}+\|v\|_{H^{k+1}})+\sigma^{2}K\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{k+2}\big).\end{array}

(4.99)

Proof.

Inspecting the representation
(
4.94
),
this bound follows from
(
4.29
)
and Corollary

4.20
.
∎

4.5
Low dimensional bounds

In this section, we consider the case
1\leq d\leq 4

and search for bounds in
H^{1}
.
We will make frequent use of the Sobolev embeddings

H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{4},\qquad H^{4/3}\hookrightarrow L^{6},\qquad H^{3/2}\hookrightarrow L^{8},

(4.100)

and

H^{3/2}\hookrightarrow W^{1,8/3},\qquad H^{7/4}\hookrightarrow L^{16},\qquad H^{2}\hookrightarrow W^{1,4}.

(4.101)

In addition, we often encounter the interpolation bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|v\|_{H^{4/3}}&\leq&K\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2/3}\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{1/3},\\[5.69046pt]
\|v\|_{H^{3/2}}&\leq&K\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{1/2},\\[5.69046pt]
\|v\|_{H^{7/4}}&\leq&K\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{1/4}\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{3/4}.\end{array}

(4.102)

Lemma 4.22
.

Pick
1\leq d\leq 4
, assume that
\Phi
is bounded with
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{2}
, and consider a
C^{1}
-smooth function
\Theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
for which
D^{\ell}\Theta
is globally Lipschitz for all
0\leq\ell\leq 1
.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
so that
for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{3/2}
we have the bound

\|\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq K(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{3/2}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{3/2}}.

(4.103)

If in addition
v_{B}\in H^{2}
, then we have

\|\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq K(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{2}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}}.

(4.104)

Proof.

The bound is clear in
L^{2}
. Inspecting the pointwise estimate (
4.2
),
the bound (
4.103
) follows from applying Hölder’s inequality which yields

\|(v_{A}-v_{B})v_{B}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|(v_{A}-v_{B})\|_{L^{8}}\|v_{B}^{\prime}\|_{L^{8/3}}

(4.105)

and by appealing to the Sobolev embeddings
(
4.100
)–(
4.101
).
On the other hand, the estimate (
4.104
)
follows from

\|(v_{A}-v_{B})v_{B}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{4}}\|v_{B}^{\prime}\|_{L^{4}}

(4.106)

and using the embeddings
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{4}

and
H^{2}\hookrightarrow W^{1,4}
.
∎

Observe, in the next result we are restricting
our Lipschitz estimate to the special case
v_{A}=v
and

v_{B}=0
. This is on account
of the pointwise term
|v_{A}-v_{B}||\partial^{\gamma_{1}}v_{B}||\partial^{\gamma_{2}}v_{B}|
arising from the third line of (
4.109
),
which requires higher order norms.

Lemma 4.23
.

Pick
1\leq d\leq 4
, assume that
\Phi
is bounded with
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{2}
, and consider a
C^{2}
-smooth function
\Theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
for which
D^{\ell}\Theta
is globally Lipschitz for all
0\leq\ell\leq 2
.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
so that
for any
v\in H^{2}
we have the bound

\|\Theta(\Phi+v)-\Theta(\Phi)\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq K(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}.

(4.107)

Proof.

Pick two multi-indices
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{\geq 0}
with
|\gamma_{1}|=|\gamma_{2}|=1
. Writing

\Delta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\Theta=\partial^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\big[\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})\big],

(4.108)

with
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{2}
, we may compute

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\Theta&=&D^{2}\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})[\partial^{\gamma_{1}}\Phi+\partial^{\gamma_{1}}v_{A}][\partial^{\gamma_{2}}v_{A}-\partial^{\gamma_{2}}v_{B}]\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,D\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})[\partial^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}v_{A}-\partial^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}v_{B}]\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\big(D^{2}\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-D^{2}\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})\big)[\partial^{\gamma_{1}}\Phi+\partial^{\gamma_{1}}v_{B},\partial^{\gamma_{2}}\Phi+\partial^{\gamma_{2}}v_{B}]\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\big(D^{2}\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})\big)[\partial^{\gamma_{1}}v_{A}-\partial^{\gamma_{1}}v_{B},\partial^{\gamma_{2}}\Phi+\partial^{\gamma_{2}}v_{B}].\end{array}

(4.109)

Note that
D\Theta
and
D^{2}\Theta
are globally bounded, and that for any pair
w_{1},w_{2}\in H^{2}
we have

\|\partial^{\gamma_{1}}w_{1}\partial^{\gamma_{2}}w_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial^{\gamma_{1}}w_{1}\|_{L^{4}}\|\partial^{\gamma_{2}}w_{2}\|_{L^{4}}\leq\|w_{1}\|_{H^{2}}\|w_{2}\|_{H^{2}}.

(4.110)

In particular, for
v_{A}=v
and
v_{B}=0
we may
proceed term by term to compute

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\Theta\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\big[|v\|_{H^{1}}+\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|v\|_{H^{2}}+\|v\|_{L^{2}}+\|v\|_{H^{1}}\big],\end{array}

(4.111)

which can be absorbed in the stated bound.
∎

Lemma 4.24
.

Pick
1\leq d\leq 4
, assume that
\Phi
is bounded with
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{2}
, and consider a
C^{3}
-smooth function
\Theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
for which
D^{3}\Theta
is globally bounded.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
so that
for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{4/3}
we have the bound

\|\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}\leq K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{4/3}}^{2}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{4/3}}^{2})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{4/3}}.

(4.112)

Proof.

In view of the pointwise bound

|\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})|\leq[1+|v_{A}|^{2}+|v_{B}|^{2}]|v_{A}-v_{B}|,

(4.113)

we may compute

\displaystyle\|\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq K[1+\|v_{A}\|^{2}_{L^{6}}+\|v_{B}\|^{2}_{L^{6}}]\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{6}},

(4.114)

which leads to the stated estimate using the embedding
H^{4/3}\hookrightarrow L^{6}
.
∎

Lemma 4.25
.

Pick
1\leq d\leq 4
, assume that
\Phi
is bounded with
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{2}
, and consider a
C^{3}
-smooth function
\Theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
for which
D^{3}\Theta
is globally bounded.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
so that
for any pair
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{2}
we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{A}\|_{H^{1}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{2}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+K\|v_{A}\|_{H^{7/4}}^{2}\|(v_{A}-v_{B})\|_{H^{3/2}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+K(\|v_{A}\|_{H^{7/4}}+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{7/4}})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad\qquad\times\|v_{B}\|_{H^{3/2}}\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{7/4}}.\end{array}

(4.115)

Proof.

Pick a multi-index
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{\geq 0}
with
|\gamma|=1
. Writing

\Delta_{\gamma}\Theta=\partial^{\gamma}[\Theta(\Phi+v_{A})-\Theta(\Phi+v_{B})],

(4.116)

we may inspect (
4.1
) to obtain the pointwise bound

|\Delta_{\gamma}\Theta|\leq K(1+|v_{A}|^{2})|\partial^{\gamma}v_{A}-\partial^{\gamma}v_{B}|+K(1+|v_{A}|+|v_{B}|)|v_{A}-v_{B}|(|\partial^{\gamma}\Phi|+|\partial^{\gamma}v_{B}|).

(4.117)

Proceeding term by term, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{\gamma}\Theta\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}}+K\|v_{A}\|_{L^{16}}^{2}\|\partial^{\gamma}(v_{A}-v_{B})\|_{L^{8/3}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+K(\|v_{A}\|_{L^{4}}+\|v_{B}\|_{L^{4}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{4}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{4}}\|\partial^{\gamma}v_{B}\|_{L^{4}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+K(\|v_{A}\|_{L^{16}}+\|v_{B}\|_{L^{16}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{16}}\|\partial^{\gamma}v_{B}\|_{L^{8/3}},\end{array}

(4.118)

which leads to the stated bound upon applying
the Sobolev embeddings (
4.100
)–(
4.101
).
∎

Corollary 4.26
.

Pick
1\leq d\leq 4
, assume that
\Phi
is bounded with
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{2}
, and consider a
C^{3}
-smooth function
\Theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
for which
D^{3}\Theta
is globally bounded.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
so that
for any
v\in H^{2}
we have the bound

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Theta(\Phi+v)-\Theta(\Phi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\|v\|_{H^{2}}\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big),\\[5.69046pt]
\|\Theta(\Phi+v)-\Theta(\Phi)\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\|v\|_{H^{2}}\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}+\|v\|_{H^{1}}\|v\|_{H^{2}}\big).\end{array}

(4.119)

Proof.

Applying the interpolation estimates (
4.102
)
to the bound (
4.112
) with
v_{A}=v
and
v_{B}=0

leads directly to the first estimate. Performing the same
for (
4.115
), we find

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Theta(\Phi+v)-\Theta(\Phi)\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\|v\|_{H^{1}}+K\|v\|_{H^{1}}\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+K\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2},\end{array}

(4.120)

which can be absorbed in the stated bound.
∎

Lemma 4.27
.

Pick
1\leq d\leq 4
, assume that
\Phi
is bounded with
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{2}
, and consider a
C^{3}
-smooth function
\Theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
for which
D^{3}\Theta
is globally bounded.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
so that
for any
v\in H^{2}
we have the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Theta(\Phi+v)-\Theta(\Phi)-D\Theta(\Phi)[v]\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2},\\[5.69046pt]
\|\Theta(\Phi+v)-\Theta(\Phi)-D\Theta(\Phi)[v]\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2}.\end{array}

(4.121)

Proof.

Recall the identity (
4.12
). Writing

\mathcal{Q}=D^{2}(\Phi+stv)[v,v],

(4.122)

for
0\leq s,t\leq 1,
and picking a multi-index
\gamma
with
|\gamma|=1
,
we first compute

\partial^{\gamma}\mathcal{Q}=2D^{2}\Theta(\Phi+stv)[\partial^{\gamma}v,v]+D^{3}\Theta(\Phi+stv)[\partial^{\gamma}\Phi+st\partial^{\gamma}v,v,v],

(4.123)

which provides the pointwise bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}|\mathcal{Q}|&\leq&K(1+|v|)|v|^{2},\\[5.69046pt]
|\partial^{\gamma}\mathcal{Q}|&\leq&K(1+|v|)|v||\partial^{\gamma}v|+K(1+|\partial^{\gamma}v|)|v|^{2}\leq K|v|\big(|v|+|\partial^{\gamma}v|+|v||\partial^{\gamma}v|\big).\end{array}

(4.124)

In particular, we find

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\|v\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+K\|v\|_{L^{6}}^{3},\\[5.69046pt]
\|\partial^{\gamma}\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\|v\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+K\|v\|_{L^{8}}\|\partial^{\gamma}v\|_{L^{8/3}}+K\|v\|_{L^{16}}^{2}\|\partial^{\gamma}v\|_{L^{8/3}},\end{array}

(4.125)

which in view of the Sobolev embeddings (
4.100
)–(
4.101
)
leads to the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\Big(\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|v\|_{H^{4/3}}^{3}\Big),\\[5.69046pt]
\|\partial^{\gamma}Q\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{N})}&\leq&K\Big(\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|v\|_{H^{3/2}}\|v\|_{H^{3/2}}+\|v\|_{H^{7/4}}^{2}\|v\|_{H^{3/2}}\Big).\end{array}

(4.126)

The stated estimates now follow by applying the
interpolation bounds (
4.102
).
∎

Corollary 4.28
.

Suppose that
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied with
k=1

and pick a multi-index
\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{\geq 0}
with

|\beta|=1
.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
,
which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
v\in H^{2}
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
we have the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)-\partial^{\beta}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{C}(\Phi,\gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{Q}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{1}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi+v,\gamma)-\mathcal{K}_{C}(\Phi,\gamma)\|_{H^{2}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{3})(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}.\end{array}

(4.127)

Proof.

The first bound follows from (
4.62
) and by inspecting (
4.37
) with
\gamma=\gamma_{A}=\gamma_{B}
,
v_{A}=v
and
v_{B}=0
.
To obtain the second bound, we again consider the splitting in (
4.82
), but now with
v_{A}=v
and
v_{B}=0
.
Lemma
4.8

together with (
4.107
)
yields

\|\mathcal{E}_{I}\|_{H^{1}}\leq K\|v\|_{L^{2}}(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2})+K(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}.

(4.128)

whereas (
4.104
) together with the
first estimate of (
4.127
)
allows us to find

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{E}_{II}\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&K\|v\|_{L^{2}}(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}})(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K\|v\|_{H^{1}}(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}})(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})(1+\|v\|_{L^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{1}}.\end{array}

(4.129)

Note that both bounds can be absorbed by the stated estimate.
∎

Thanks to the preparations above, we are now ready to work towards
the final estimates for
\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}
. To this end,
we recall the expressions
\Xi_{I}
and
\Xi_{II;\sigma}
defined in (
4.87
), together with
the intermediate function
\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}

and the associated decomposition (
4.96
).

Corollary 4.29
.

Suppose that
(Hf-Cub)
,
(HCor)
,
(Hq)
,
(HSt)
and
(HPar)
are satisfied
with
k=1
.
Then there exists a constant
K>0
,
which does not
depend on the pair
(\Phi,c)
, so that the following holds true.
For any
v\in H^{2}
, any
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, and any
\sigma\geq 0
,
the expressions

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{v}\Xi_{I}&=&\Xi_{I}(\Phi+v,c)-\Xi_{I}(\Phi,c),\\[5.69046pt]
\Delta_{v}\Xi_{II;\sigma}&=&\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi+v,\gamma)-\Xi_{II;\sigma}(\Phi,\gamma)\end{array}

(4.130)

satisfy the bounds

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\Delta_{v}\Xi_{I},\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2})\|v\|_{H^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\langle\Delta_{v}\Xi_{II;\sigma},\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&\sigma^{2}K\|v\|_{L^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\end{array}

(4.131)

together with

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\Delta_{v}\Xi_{I}\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}+\|v\|_{H^{1}}\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|\Delta_{v}\Xi_{II;\sigma}\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&\sigma^{2}K\big(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{3})(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}.\end{array}

(4.132)

Proof.

These bounds readily follow by inspecting the definitions (
4.87
)
and applying (
4.25
),
(
4.104
),
(
4.119
)
and
(
4.127
).
∎

Corollary 4.30
.

Suppose that
(Hf-Cub)
,
(HCor)
,
(Hq)
and
(HSt)
are satisfied
with
k=1
.
Then there exists a constant
K>0

so that for all sufficiently small
\sigma\geq 0

and all
v\in H^{2}

we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v),\psi_{\rm tw}\rangle_{L^{2}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2})\|v\|_{H^{2}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v)\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{3})(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}.\end{array}

(4.133)

Proof.

The
L^{2}
-bound follows from
Lemma
4.10
,
Corollary
4.29

and the quadratic estimate (
4.121
).
Similar computations for the
H^{1}
-bound lead to the
estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{N}_{I;\sigma}(v)\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\sigma^{2}K\|v\|_{L^{2}}(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}+\|v\|_{H^{1}}\|v\|_{H^{2}})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{3})(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}},\end{array}

(4.134)

which can be absorbed in the stated bound.
∎

Corollary 4.31
.

Suppose that
(Hf-Cub)
,
(HCor)
,
(Hq)
and
(HSt)
are satisfied
with
k=1
.
Then there exists a constant
K>0

so that for all sufficiently small
\sigma\geq 0

and all
v\in H^{2}

we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(v)\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2})\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{3})(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}.\end{array}

(4.135)

Proof.

By inspecting the representation
(
4.94
),
we see that Corollary
4.30

together with
(
4.29
)
lead to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(v)\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{3})(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{2}}\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,K(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}})\\[5.69046pt]
&&\qquad+\,\sigma^{2}K(1+\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2})\|v\|_{H^{2}}(1+\|v\|_{H^{2}}),\end{array}

(4.136)

which can be absorbed in the stated bound.
∎

5
Variational solutions

In this section, we establish Proposition
2.2
and study the regularity of the obtained solutions. In particular, we provide existence and uniqueness results for the coupled SPDE-SDE system

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}u=[\Delta u+f(u)+\sigma^{2}h(u)]\mathrm{d}t+\sigma g(u)\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{Q},\\
\mathrm{d}\gamma=[c+a_{\sigma}(u,\gamma;c)]\mathrm{d}t+\sigma b(u,\gamma)\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{Q},\end{cases}

(5.1)

where
u
takes values in the affine space
\mathcal{U}_{H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}
, i.e.,
u=\Phi_{\mathrm{ref}}+v
with
v\in H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
, for some
k\geq 0
.
We start by considering global existence for the case

k=0
in §
5.1
and move on to study local existence for
k>d/2
in §
5.2
, all under our global Lipschitz assumption (Hf-Lip).
We provide an alternative approach
for global existence results in §
5.3
that works for
k=1
and
1\leq d\leq 4
under the cubic growth condition (Hf-Cub).

Our main findings beyond the
L^{2}
-based statements in Proposition
2.2
are summarised in the following result.
The precise interpretation of the diffusion operator in the identity
(
5.4
) is discussed throughout this section. We emphasise that the various types of solutions that we construct agree where they overlap; see, e.g., Lemma
5.2
below.

Proposition 5.1

(Higher regularity)
.

Suppose that either
(Hf-Lip)
is satisfied with
k>d/2
or
that
k=1
and
(Hf-Cub)
is satisfied. Assume furthermore that

(HCor)
,
(Hq)
and
(HSt)
are satisfied.
Fix
T>0
and
0\leq\sigma\leq 1
. Then for any initial condition

(z_{0},\gamma_{0})\in H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R},

(5.2)

there exists an increasing sequence of stopping times
(\tau_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 0}
and a stopping time
\tau_{\infty}
, with
\tau_{\ell}\to\tau_{\infty}
and
0<\tau_{\infty}\leq T

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., together with progressively measurable maps

z:[0,T]\times\Omega\to H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\gamma:[0,T]\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R},

(5.3)

that satisfy the following properties:

(i)

For almost every
\omega\in\Omega
, the map

t\mapsto(z(t,\omega),\gamma(t,\omega))

is of class
C([0,\tau_{\infty}(\omega));H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R})
;

(ii)

We have the integrability condition
(z,\gamma)\in L^{2}(\Omega;L^{2}([0,\tau_{\ell}];H^{k+1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R}))
, for any
\ell\geq 0
;

(iii)

The
H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
-valued identity
11
11

11

At first, the equality in (
5.4
) should be understood as an equality in
[H^{k+1}]^{*}\equiv H^{k-1}
, but by (ii) we can conclude that we have equality in
H^{k}
.

\displaystyle z(t)=z_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}[\Delta z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref}^{\prime\prime}]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}f(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref})\mathrm{d}s

(5.4)

\displaystyle+\sigma^{2}\int_{0}^{t}h(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref})\mathrm{d}s+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}g(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref})\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{Q},

together with the scalar identity

\gamma(t)=\gamma_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}[c+a_{\sigma}(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref},\gamma(s);c)]\mathrm{d}s+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}b(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref},\gamma(s))\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{Q},

(5.5)

hold
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
0\leq t<\tau_{\infty}
;

(iv)

Suppose there are other progressively measurable maps
\tilde{z}
and
\tilde{\gamma}
that satisfy
(i)–(iii)
with another stopping time
\tilde{\tau}_{\infty}
and localising sequence
(\tilde{\tau}_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 0}
. Then for almost all
\omega\in\Omega,
we have
\tilde{\tau}_{\infty}(\omega)\leq\tau_{\infty}(\omega)
together with

\quad\tilde{z}(t,\omega)=z(t,\omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\gamma}(t,\omega)=\gamma(t,\omega),\quad\text{for all}\quad 0\leq t\leq\tilde{\tau}_{\infty}(\omega).

(5.6)

In the setting where
k=1
and
(Hf-Cub)
is satisfied, we may take

\tau_{\ell}=\tau_{\infty}=T
.

5.1
Global existence in
L^{2}

Throughout this part, we consider
k=0
and solutions where
(v,\gamma)
is measured with respect to the Gelfand triple
(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}^{*})
given by

\mathcal{V}=H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R},\quad\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R},\quad\mathcal{V}^{*}=H^{-1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R}.

(5.7)

The associated inner products are given by

\displaystyle\langle(v,\gamma_{A}),(w,\gamma_{B})\rangle_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle=\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\langle\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\langle(v,\gamma_{A}),(w,\gamma_{B})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\langle v,w\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\langle\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}},

while the duality pairing acts as

\langle(v,\gamma_{A}),(w,\gamma_{B})\rangle_{\mathcal{V}^{*};\mathcal{V}}=\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{-1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\langle\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}},

(5.9)

where we follow
[
58
]
to interpret
the duality pairing between the spaces
H^{-1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
In particular, for any
v\in L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})

and
w\in H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
we have

\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{-1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\langle v,w\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(5.10)

In addition, the Laplacian can be interpreted as a bounded linear operator mapping from
H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
into
H^{-1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
by writing

\langle\Delta v,w\rangle_{H^{-1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n});H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}=-\langle\nabla v,\nabla w\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}=-\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\langle v,w\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(5.11)

for any pair
v,w\in H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
In the parlance of
[
4
]
, this is analogous to the so-called
weak setting
.

We are now ready to prove Proposition
2.2
,
primarily using the estimates for
a_{\sigma}
and
b

obtained in Corollary
4.9

and Lemma
4.11
. As a consequence
of the global Lipschitz assumption on
f
, these
estimates allow us to establish Proposition
2.2
in a more direct manner than the approach in
[
27
,
30
]
.
Indeed, we are able to directly embed the coupled system (
5.1
) within a variational framework,
rather than solving first for
u

and then interpreting the equation for
\gamma
as an SDE with random coefficients.

It is worthwhile to point out that
we can either use the classical variational framework
[
58
]
of Liu and Röckner or the critical variational framework
[
4
]
developed by Agresti and Veraar. Indeed, the Lipschitz estimates
(
4.42
) and
(
4.49
)
do not depend on both
v_{A}
and
v_{B}
simultaneously and only involve
L^{2}
-norms, enabling us to verify the local monotonicity condition
required by
[
58
]
for the full coupled
system (
5.1
).

Proof of Proposition
2.2
under
(Hf-Lip)
.

It suffices to check the conditions in
[
58
, Thm. 1.1]
, which provides our statements when applied with
\alpha=2
and
\beta=2
.
We shall merely focus on the parts regarding the local monotonicity, coercivity, and the growth condition induced by the SDE of
\gamma
. The remaining conditions can be readily verified by the reader by exploiting the identity (
5.11
)
and appealing to Lemma
4.6
and Corollary
4.7
.

Using the bounds in Lemma
4.11
, we see for any
v_{A},v_{B}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
and any
\gamma_{A},\gamma_{B}\in\mathbb{R}
that

\displaystyle\langle a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A},c)

\displaystyle-a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B},c),\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq K_{a}\rho(v_{A})\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D}:\mathbb{R}^{n})}+|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|\big]|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{a}\rho(v_{A})\big[\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D}:\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}+|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|^{2}\big],

in which we have exploited the scalar identity
xy\leq x^{2}+y^{2}
and introduced the function

\rho(v)=\big[1+\|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\big]^{2}.

(5.13)

In addition, for any
v\in H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
we
may use the scalar identity above again together with
x\leq 1+x^{2}
to conclude

\displaystyle\langle a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v,\gamma,c),\gamma\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\leq|a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v,\gamma,c)\|\gamma|

(5.14)

\displaystyle\leq K_{a}\big[1+\|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\big]|\gamma|

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{a}\big[1+\|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}+|\gamma|^{2}\big].

The second estimate in (
4.42
) together with the bound in (
5.12
) now yield the local monotonicity property
[
58
, (H2)]
, whereas the coercivity condition
[
58
, (H3)]
and the growth condition
[
58
, (H4)]
follow from (
4.40
) and (
5.14
).
∎

5.2
Local existence in
H^{k}

In this part we investigate the regularity of the solution found in Proposition
2.2
, which we refer to as
(\tilde{z},\tilde{\gamma})\in L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R}
. We do this by establishing the (local) existence and uniqueness of solutions in
H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R}
, which we write as
(z,\gamma).
If
z
has an initial value in
H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
, then it remains in
H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
for at least a short time.
In particular, we show that
(z,\gamma)
coincides with the solution
(\tilde{z},\tilde{\gamma})
, where they overlap, showing that
z
persists globally as a continuous
L^{2}
-valued solution even after the
H^{k}
-smoothness is lost.

Indeed, one can follow

[
4
,
7
,
48
]
to show
that for
k\geq 1
the bilinear map

\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}=\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{k-1}}-\sum_{|\alpha|=k-1}\langle\partial^{\alpha}v,\partial^{\alpha}\Delta w\rangle_{L^{2}}

(5.15)

allows
H^{k-1}

to be interpreted as the dual of
H^{k+1}
. In order to confirm that this duality pairing is compatible with the inner product of
H^{k}
, it suffices to compute

\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}=\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{k-1}}+\sum_{|\alpha|=k-1}\langle\partial^{\alpha}\nabla v,\partial^{\alpha}\nabla w\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{k}},

(5.16)

whenever
v\in H^{k}

and
w\in H^{k+1}
.
In addition, the diffusion operator
\Delta
can—as usual—be seen as an element of
\mathscr{L}(H^{k+1};H^{k-1})
.
The definition (
5.15
)
yields

\langle\Delta v,w\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}=-\langle\nabla v,\nabla w\rangle_{H^{k}}=-\langle v,w\rangle_{H^{k+1}}+\langle v,w\rangle_{L^{2}}

(5.17)

for any pair
v,w\in H^{k+1}
,
hence generalising (
5.11
) and providing an alternative yet equivalent definition for
\Delta
from the space
H^{k+1}
into its dual.

As explained in the introduction, the presence of derivatives generates cross terms
that violate the monotonicity requirements in
[
58
]
; see e.g., Lemma
4.16
.
We therefore appeal to the critical variational framework
[
4
]
instead. We remark that the proof below only requires the
C^{k}
-Lipschitz smoothness on both
f
and
g
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
under
(Hf-Lip)
.

For any integer
n\in\mathbb{N},

the bounds in
Corollaries
4.13

and
4.16

allow us to find constants

C_{1,n},C_{2,n}>0
for which
the estimates

\|f(\Phi+v_{A})-f(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{k-1}}+\|h(\Phi+v_{A})-h(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{k-1}}\leq C_{1,n}\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}},

(5.18)

and

\|g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k})}\leq C_{2,n}\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}

(5.19)

hold whenever
\|v_{A}\|_{H^{k}},\|v_{B}\|_{H^{k}}\leq n
.
Together with the fact that (
5.17
) implies

\langle\Delta v,v\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}\leq-\|v\|_{H^{k+1}}^{2}+\|v\|_{H^{k}}^{2},

(5.20)

and recalling the estimates related to the
\gamma
-variable
obtained in the proof of Proposition
2.2
,
we note that the result follows by appealing to

[
4
, Thm 3.3]
.
In particular, the latter yields the existence and uniqueness of a maximal solution
((z,\gamma),\sigma_{\infty})
with a corresponding localising sequence
(\sigma_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 0}
. Upon
defining the stopping times

\tau_{\ell}=\sigma_{\ell}\wedge\inf\{t\geq 0:\sup_{t\geq 0}\|z(t)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|z(s)\|_{H^{k+1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}|\gamma(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s>\ell\},

(5.21)

the blow up criterion of
\sigma_{\infty}
in
[
4
, Thm 3.3]
shows that
\tau_{\ell}\to\sigma_{\infty}
holds. We may hence set
\tau_{\infty}=\sigma_{\infty}
,
from which all the claims follow.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Consider the setting of Proposition
5.1
where
(Hf-Lip)
is satisfied.
Writing

(\tilde{z},\tilde{\gamma})
for the
L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R})\times\mathbb{R}
-valued solution constructed in Proposition

2.2
, we have

\tilde{z}(t,\omega)=z(t,\omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\gamma}(t,\omega)=\gamma(t,\omega),

(5.22)

for all
0\leq t<\tau_{\infty}(\omega)
and almost every
\omega\in\Omega
.

Proof.

Without loss, set
h=0.
Although we can identify the identity in (
5.4
) as an equality in
H^{k}
, it is actually an equality in
[H^{k+1}]^{*}
, which we will now exploit.
Indeed, for all
\zeta\in H^{k+1}
we have

\displaystyle\langle z(t),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}

\displaystyle=\langle z_{0},\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\Delta z(s),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\langle f(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref}),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}\mathrm{d}s

(5.23)

\displaystyle+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\langle g(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref})\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{Q},\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}.

Picking an arbitrary
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we write
\zeta=(1-\Delta)^{-k}\eta
and note that
\zeta\in H^{\ell}
for any
\ell\geq 0
. For any
v\in H^{k}
, Parseval’s identity (see Appendix
C
) yields

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle v,\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}&=&\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}\mapsto(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2})^{k/2}\hat{v}(\boldsymbol{\xi}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\mapsto(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2})^{k/2}\hat{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\rangle_{L^{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\\[5.69046pt]
&=&\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}\mapsto\hat{v}(\boldsymbol{\xi}),\boldsymbol{\xi}\mapsto(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2})^{k}\hat{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\rangle_{L^{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\\[5.69046pt]
&=&\langle v,(1-\Delta)^{k}\zeta\rangle_{L^{2}}\\[5.69046pt]
&=&\langle v,\eta\rangle_{L^{2}}.\end{array}

(5.24)

In addition, we have the pathwise identities

\langle\Delta z(s),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}=\langle z(s),\Delta\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}=\langle z(s),\Delta\eta\rangle_{L^{2}},

(5.25)

for any
0\leq s<\tau_{\infty}
. We find that
z(t)
is an analytically weak solution in
L^{2},
i.e.,

\displaystyle\langle z(t),\eta\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle z_{0},\eta\rangle_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle z(s),\Delta\eta\rangle_{L^{2}}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\langle f(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref}),\eta\rangle_{L^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

(5.26)

\displaystyle+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\langle g(z(s)+\Phi_{\rm ref})\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{Q},\eta\rangle_{L^{2}},

and conclude that
z(t)
is a solution in the sense of Proposition
2.2
by invoking a standard density argument. The fact that
\gamma(t)
and
\tilde{\gamma}(t)
coincide directly follows.
∎

5.3
Global existence in
H^{1}
for
1\leq d\leq 4

We conclude by considering an alternative approach towards global existence that is valid in low spatial dimensions, namely
1\leq d\leq 4
. The relevant Gelfand triple is given by

\mathcal{V}=H^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R},\quad\mathcal{H}=H^{1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R},\quad\mathcal{V}^{*}=L^{2}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{R},

(5.27)

which is analogous to the
strong setting

in
[
4
]
.
The duality pairings are the same as those in §
5.2

upon fixing
k=1
and we consider the cubic growth condition (Hf-Cub).

For spatial dimension
d=1
, the classical framework

[
58
]
can be used to construct
solutions to (
5.1
)
in the weak setting (
5.7
),
under the additional variational condition (
1.16
)
[
27
,
30
]
.
However, the Sobolev embeddings simply do not work out in
d>1
, while the analogue of (
1.16
)
fails in the strong setting (
5.27
).
Fortunately, such an inequality is not needed
for the approach developed in the critical variational framework
[
4
]
.

Let us point out that we cannot solve
(z,\gamma)
in one go as in the previous settings. This is because the estimate
(
5.29
)
for
f
below implies

\displaystyle|a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v_{A},\gamma_{A},c)-a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v_{B},\gamma_{B},c)|\leq

\displaystyle K\big[1+\|v_{A}\|^{2}_{H^{4/3}}+\|v_{B}\|^{2}_{H^{4/3}}\big]\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{4/3}}

(5.28)

\displaystyle+K\big[1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{4/3}}^{3}\big]|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|,

which does not satisfy Assumption 3.1 in
[
4
]
due to the cubic growth term.
Nevertheless, we can solve for
z
first and follow the random-coefficient approach developed in
[
27
,
30
]
to understand
\gamma
.

We remark that within this setting the dimension restriction
1\leq d\leq 4
is induced by both the nonlinearity
f
and the noise term
g
. It is related
to the critical exponents
4/3
and
3/2
that appear
in
[
4
]

and the proof below.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
under
(Hf-Cub)
.

As before, the estimates pertaining to the
\gamma
-variable
in the proof of Proposition
2.2
also suffice here, so we only need to focus on the terms related to the SPDE of
z
.
Without loss, we take
h=0
again.
On account of (Hf-Cub), Lemma
4.24

provides the bound

\displaystyle\|f(\Phi+v_{A})-f(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}[1+\|v_{A}\|^{2}_{H^{4/3}}+\|v_{B}\|^{2}_{H^{4/3}}]\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{4/3}},

(5.29)

which involves the exponent
4/3
that is critical in
[
4
]
.
In addition, Lemmas
4.5

and
4.22

together yield the estimate

\|g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{1})}\leq C_{2}(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{3/2}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{3/2}},

(5.30)

where again the value
3/2
is critical.
The remaining conditions needed to invoke
[
4
, Thm. 3.4]
for the first equation
in (
5.1
) can be readily verified.
As a result, we may write
u=\Phi_{\mathrm{ref}}+z

for the solution to this equation,
and conclude that
z=(z(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
lives in the Bochner spaces

C([0,T];H^{1})\quad\text{and}\quad L^{2}([0,T];H^{2}).

(5.31)

Using the interpolation bound
\|v\|_{H^{4/3}}\leq\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2/3}\|v\|_{H^{2}}^{1/3}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|z(t)\|_{H^{4/3}}^{3}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|z\|_{C([0,T];H^{1})}\int_{0}^{T}\|z(t)\|_{H^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t

(5.32)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T}\|z\|_{C([0,T];H^{1})}\|z\|_{L^{2}([0,T];H^{2})},

which implies
z\in L^{3}([0,T];H^{4/3})
.
In particular, the bound
(
5.28
) yields

\displaystyle|a_{\sigma}(\Phi+z(t),\gamma_{A},c)-a_{\sigma}(\Phi+z(t),\gamma_{B},c)|\leq K\rho\big(z(t)\big)|\gamma_{A}-\gamma_{B}|

(5.33)

with the weight function

\rho(v)=1+\|v\|_{H^{4/3}}^{3}.

(5.34)

Note that
t\mapsto\rho\big(z(t)\big)
is integrable.
This allows us to follow the approach
in the proof of
[
30
, Prop. 4.5.2]
—which refers to
[
73
, Ch. 3]
—to
establish the global existence and uniqueness of
\gamma(t)
.
∎

We remark that under the weaker condition (Hf-Lip),
the same result can be obtained by simply
applying the classical variational framework
[
58
]

directly to the full problem (
5.1
).
In particular, this approach uses the estimates

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|f(\Phi+v_{A})-f(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{H^{1}}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{2}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}},\\[5.69046pt]
\|g(\Phi+v_{A})-g(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{1})}&\leq&K(1+\|v_{B}\|_{H^{2}})\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{H^{1}},\end{array}

(5.35)

that follow from
Lemma
4.22
and are again valid for
1\leq d\leq 4
only. Alternatively, one can invoke the framework
in
[
4
]
, for which it suffices to use
the bound

\|f(\Phi+v_{A})-f(\Phi+v_{B})\|_{L^{2}}\leq K\|v_{A}-v_{B}\|_{L^{2},}

(5.36)

in combination with (
5.30
).

Proof of Proposition
2.2
under
(Hf-Cub)
.

This is simply a restatement of the global
H^{1}
-results
in Proposition
5.1
.
∎

6
Evolution equations of the perturbation

In this section, we establish equations for the evolution of the perturbation

v(t)=T_{-\gamma(t)}u(t)-\Phi=T_{-\gamma(t)}[z(t)+\Phi_{\rm ref}]-\Phi,

(6.1)

where
z(t)
and
\gamma(t)
were constructed in §
5
. In §
6.1
we show that
v(t)
satisfies

\mathrm{d}v=\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}(v;c,\Phi)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathcal{S}(v;\Phi)\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{Q},

(6.2)

in the variational
sense, where the nonlinearities
\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{S}
are given by

\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}(v;c,\Phi)=\Delta_{y}v+\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi+v,0)\big[\partial_{x}^{2}v+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(\Phi+v,0;c)\big]+a_{\sigma}(\Phi+v,0,c)\partial_{x}(\Phi+v)

(6.3)

and

\mathcal{S}(v;\Phi)[\xi]=g(\Phi+v)[\xi]+\partial_{x}(\Phi+v)b(\Phi+v,0)[\xi],\quad\xi\in L^{2}_{Q};

(6.4)

see Appendix
A
for the definitions of
a_{\sigma}
,
b
,
\kappa_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}
, which are analogous to the
d=1
case
and do not involve second derivatives.

It is worthwhile to point out that
v(t)
explicitly depends on the phase
\gamma(t)
, yet
\gamma(t)
is absent in the equations above. This is a consequence of the translational invariance of our system, which allows us to reduce the coupled system (
5.1
) to a single system of equations; see (
A.25
)–(
A.26
).

Subsequently, in §
6.2
we consider a stochastic time transformation to change the coefficient in front of
\partial_{x}^{2}
into unity; as in
\mathcal{L}_{\rm tw}
. In particular, we show that the
transformed function
\bar{v}(t)
satisfies the system

\mathrm{d}\bar{v}=[\mathcal{L}_{\rm tw}\bar{v}+\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi+\bar{v},0)^{-1}\Delta_{y}\bar{v}+\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})]\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})\mathrm{d}{W}_{t}^{Q},

(6.5)

where the definitions of
\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}

can be found in (
A.28
). As before, note that these functions do not contain second derivatives and are analogous to the
d=1
case. The main message is that we have cleanly isolated
the linear operator
\mathcal{L}_{\rm tw}
, allowing us to pass to a mild formulation where we can exploit the semigroup
S_{\rm tw}(t)
.
Indeed, in §
6.3
we show that the variational solution to (
6.5
) is also a mild solution in some sense, however, the time-dependent coefficient in front of the Laplacian
\Delta_{y}
forces us to consider random evolution families, causing significant complications for our
d>1
case that were absent in
[
27
,
30
]
.

6.1
An application of Itô’s formula

Our starting point here is the local
H^{k}
-valued solutions
constructed in Proposition
5.1
,
which we will use to
provide a rigorous interpretation
for (
6.2
). To this end,
we note that the second derivatives in
\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}

can be interpreted as an element in
\mathscr{L}(H^{k+1},H^{k-1})

in the usual sense, which corresponds with the duality pairing

\langle[\Delta_{y}+\kappa\partial_{x}^{2}]v,w\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}=-\langle\nabla_{y}v,\nabla_{y}w\rangle_{H^{k}}-\kappa\langle\partial_{x}v,\partial_{x}w\rangle_{H^{k}},

(6.6)

for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
,
v\in H^{k+1}
, and
w\in H^{k+1}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Consider the setting of Proposition
5.1
and suppose that condition
(HPar)
holds. Then the map

v:[0,T]\times\Omega\to H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})

(6.7)

defined in (
6.1
) is progressively measurable and satisfies the following properties:

(i)

For almost every
\omega\in\Omega
, the map

t\mapsto v(t,\omega)

is of class
C([0,\tau_{\infty}(\omega));H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n}))
;

(ii)

For any
\ell\geq 0
we have the integrability condition
v\in L^{2}(\Omega;L^{2}([0,\tau_{\ell}];H^{k+1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n}))
;

(iii)

For almost every
\omega\in\Omega
and any
\ell\geq 0
, we have
\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}(v(\cdot,\omega);c,\Phi)\in L^{1}([0,\tau_{\ell}(\omega)];H^{k-1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, together with
\mathcal{S}(v;\Phi)\in L^{2}(\Omega;L^{2}([0,\tau_{\ell}];HS(L^{2}_{Q};H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})))
;

(iv)

The
H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
-valued identity

v(t)=v(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}(v(s);c,\Phi)\mathrm{d}s+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{S}(v(s);\Phi)\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{Q},

(6.8)

holds
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
0\leq t<\tau_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Properties (i)–(iii) follow rather directly from Proposition
5.1
. The proof of (iv) is completely analogous to that of in
[
30
, Sec. 5.4]
, because an application of Itô’s formula only results into terms with derivatives with respect to the direction of the wave, since
\gamma(t)
only affects the
x
-coordinate.
In particular, the Laplacian with respect to
y
introduces no unexpected terms.

In more detail, we pick an arbitrary test function
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
and consider the maps

\psi_{1;\zeta}:H^{k-1}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R},\quad\psi_{2;\zeta}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R},

(6.9)

defined by

\psi_{1;\zeta}(z,\gamma)=\langle z,T_{\gamma}\zeta\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}

(6.10)

and

\psi_{2;\zeta}(z,\gamma)=\langle\Phi_{\rm ref}-T_{\gamma}\Phi_{\sigma},T_{\gamma}\zeta\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}=\langle\Phi_{\rm ref}-T_{\gamma}\Phi_{\sigma},T_{\gamma}\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}.

(6.11)

By construction, we have the identity

\langle v(t),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}=\psi_{1;\zeta}(z(t),\gamma(t))+\psi_{2;\zeta}(\gamma(t)).

(6.12)

Performing computations as in
[
30
, Lem. 5.5]
and
[
30
, Cor. 5.6]
leads to the expression

\langle v(t),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}=\langle v(0),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}(v(s);c,\Phi),\zeta\rangle_{H^{k-1};H^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}s+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathcal{S}(v(s);\Phi)\mathrm{d}\widetilde{W}_{s}^{Q},\zeta\rangle_{H^{k}},

(6.13)

where

\widetilde{W}_{t}^{Q}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}T_{-\gamma(s)}\sqrt{Q}e_{k}\mathrm{d}\beta_{k}(s)

(6.14)

is a stochastic process that is, in fact, indistinguishable from the cylindrical
Q
-Wiener process
W^{Q}_{t}
on account of the translational invariance of
Q
; see the proof of
[
30
, Prop 5.4]
for more information. By convention, we may replace the stochastic process
\widetilde{W}^{Q}_{t}
by
W^{Q}_{t}
, which proves the assertion.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

As a matter of fact, throughout the proof of Proposition
6.1
, one only needs to exploit the translation invariant property of our noise and our system in the
x
-coordinate. Therefore, it is also possible to consider other types of noise with a more general
y
-dependence.

6.2
Stochastic time transformation

Our goal here is to introduce the time transformation that will lead to the system (
6.5
). As in the one-dimensional setting
[
27
,
30
]
,
we will rescale time homogeneously over space in order
to divide out the troublesome
\kappa_{\sigma}
coefficient in front of the
\partial_{x}^{2}
term in (
6.2
). However, it will
reappear in front of the
\Delta_{y}
term, which means
that the resulting problem is still quasi-linear instead of semi-linear as in the one-dimensional setting.
Nevertheless, this repositioning is an essential part of our analysis.

Consider
v=(v(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
to be the map defined
in
(
6.1
), where we let
(z,\gamma)
be the global solution constructed
in Proposition
2.2
, and where we take

(\Phi,c)=(\Phi_{\sigma},c_{\sigma})
from this point forward.
Let us introduce the notation

\tau_{\sigma;v}(t,\omega)=\int_{0}^{t}\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+v(s,\omega),0)\,\mathrm{d}s.

(6.15)

Lemma
4.10
shows that
t\mapsto\tau_{\sigma;v}(t)
is a continuous strictly increasing
\mathbb{F}
-adapted process. This implies that it admits an inverse map
t_{\sigma;v}:[0,T]\times\Omega\to[0,T]
that satisfies

\tau_{\sigma;v}(t_{\sigma;v}(\tau,\omega),\omega)=\tau,\qquad t_{\sigma;v}(\tau_{\sigma;v}(t,\omega),\omega)=t,

(6.16)

for (almost) every
\omega\in\Omega.

Specifically, we may use (
4.46
) to find a constant
K_{\kappa}\geq 1
such that

t\leq\tau_{\sigma;v}(t)\leq K_{\kappa}t,\qquad K_{\kappa}^{-1}\tau\leq t_{\sigma;v}(\tau)\leq\tau,

(6.17)

for all
0\leq t,\tau\leq T
.

We now define the stochastic time transformed perturbation as

\bar{v}(\tau,\omega)=v(t_{\sigma;v}(\tau,\omega),\omega).

(6.18)

Applying standard time transformation rules
[
27
, Lem. 6.2]

to (
6.2
) formally leads to
the system

\mathrm{d}\bar{v}=\kappa_{\sigma}^{-1}(\Phi_{\sigma}+\bar{v},0)\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}(\bar{v};c_{\sigma},\Phi_{\sigma})\mathrm{d}\tau+\sigma\kappa_{\sigma}^{-1/2}(\Phi_{\sigma}+\bar{v},0)\mathcal{S}(\bar{v};\Phi_{\sigma}){\mathrm{d}\overline{W}}_{\tau}^{Q},

(6.19)

in which

\overline{W}_{\tau}^{Q}
is again a
Q
-cylindrical Wiener process, but now adapted to the filtration

\overline{\mathbb{F}}=(\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau})_{\tau\geq 0}
given by

\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}=\{A\in\mathcal{F}:A\cap\{t_{\sigma;v}(\tau)\leq t\}\in\mathcal{F}_{t},\,\text{for all }t\geq 0\}.

(6.20)

In particular, we have

\overline{W}^{Q}_{\tau}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sqrt{Q}e_{k}\bar{\beta}_{k}(\tau),\qquad\bar{\beta}_{k}(\tau)=\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\partial_{\tau}t_{\sigma;v}(\tau^{\prime})}}\mathrm{d}\beta_{k}(t_{\sigma;v}(\tau^{\prime})).

(6.21)

For our purposes here, it suffices to note that
\overline{W}^{Q}_{\tau}
has the same statistical properties as
W^{Q}_{t}
. Upon recalling the definitions
(
A.28
),
we see that (
6.19
) can be written in the form

\mathrm{d}\bar{v}=[\mathcal{L}_{\rm tw}\bar{v}+\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+\bar{v},0)^{-1}\Delta_{y}\bar{v}+\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})]\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})\mathrm{d}{\overline{W}}_{t}^{Q}.

(6.22)

These computations are made rigorous in the following result,
in which we have introduced the transformed stopping times

\bar{\tau}_{\ell}=\tau_{\sigma;v}(\tau_{\ell})\wedge T,\qquad\bar{\tau}_{\infty}=\tau_{\sigma;v}(\tau_{\infty})\wedge T.

(6.23)

Proposition 6.3

(stochastic time transform)
.

Consider the setting of Proposition
5.1

and suppose that condition
(HPar)
holds.
Then the map

\bar{v}:[0,T]\times\Omega\to H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})

(6.24)

defined in (
6.18
) is progressively measurable with respect to the filtration
\overline{\mathbb{F}}=(\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
in (
6.20
) and satisfies the following properties:

(i)

For almost every
\omega\in\Omega
, the map

t\mapsto\bar{v}(t,\omega)

is of class
C([0,\bar{\tau}_{\infty}(\omega));H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n}))
;

(ii)

For any
\ell\geq 0
we have the integrability condition
\bar{v}\in L^{2}(\Omega;L^{2}([0,\bar{\tau}_{\ell}];H^{k+1}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})))
;

(iii)

For almost every
\omega\in\Omega
and any
\ell\geq 0
, we have
\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\bar{v}(\cdot,\omega))\in L^{1}([0,\bar{\tau}_{\ell}(\omega)];H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, together with
\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})\in L^{2}(\Omega;L^{2}([0,\bar{\tau}_{\ell}];HS(L^{2}_{Q},H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})))
;

(iv)

The
H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
-valued identity

\displaystyle\bar{v}(t)

\displaystyle=\bar{v}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathcal{L}_{\rm tw}\bar{v}(s)+\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+\bar{v}(s),0)^{-1}\Delta_{y}\bar{v}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s

(6.25)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\bar{v}(s))\mathrm{d}s+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v}(s))\mathrm{d}\overline{W}_{s}^{Q}

holds
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
0\leq t<\bar{\tau}_{\infty}
.

Proof.

The result can be obtained by following the proof of

[
27
, Prop. 6.3]
and

[
30
, Lem. 6.3]
.
∎

6.3
Mild formulation

We are now ready to recast the system (
6.22
) into an appropriate mild formulation. The quasi-linear nature of the problem
causes several complications that need to be addressed, which we achieve by utilising the theory of forward integration discussed in §
3.2
.

Let
\bar{v}=(\bar{v}(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
denote
the stochastic process defined by (
6.18
), seen as the globally continuous
L^{2}
-valued process on account of Proposition
2.2
. Observe that this process is indistinguishable from the one in Proposition
6.3
on the interval
[0,\bar{\tau}_{\infty})
as a result of Lemma
5.2
.
Having a globally defined
\bar{v}
allows us to
introduce the random function

\nu_{\sigma}(t,\omega):=\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi_{\sigma}+\bar{v}(t,\omega),0)^{-1}

(6.26)

for all time
0\leq t\leq T.

Notice that this function is progressively measurable and continuous with respect to
t
for almost every
\omega\in\Omega
due to Lemma
4.10
and the fact

\displaystyle|\nu_{\sigma}(t)-\nu_{\sigma}(s)|=|\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi+\bar{v}(t),0)^{-1}-\kappa_{\sigma}(\Phi+\bar{v}(s),0)^{-1}|\leq K\sigma^{2}\|\bar{v}(t)-\bar{v}(s)\|_{L^{2}},\quad t,s\geq 0.

(6.27)

Indeed, recall
\bar{v}\in C([0,T],L^{2}).

In particular, property (H
\nu
) is satisfied,
with constants
K_{\nu}=1
and
k_{\nu}=\frac{1}{1+\sigma^{2}K}>0
for some
K>0.
Note that for
\sigma=0
we simply have
\nu(t)\equiv 1
.

By construction, we can now use the family of
random linear operators
\mathcal{L}_{\nu_{\sigma}}(t)

defined as in (
3.1
)
with
\nu=\nu_{\sigma}
to recast (
6.22
) into the form

\mathrm{d}\bar{v}=[\mathcal{L}_{\nu_{\sigma}}(t)\bar{v}+\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})]\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})\mathrm{d}\overline{W}_{t}^{Q}.

(6.28)

Furthermore, we denote by
E(t,s)
the associated
evolution family (
3.8
)
that features in our mild formulation below.
We emphasise that the
stochastic integral in (
6.30
) is
referred to as a generalised forward integral
and needs to be understood as
J_{2}\big(\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})\big)
;
see Corollary
3.8
.
We point out that it is unknown
whether this integral is a (strict) forward integral
in the sense of Definition
3.4
,
i.e., whether we have
I^{-}\big(E(t,\cdot)\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v}(\cdot))\big)=J_{2}\big(\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}(\bar{v})\big)
.

Proposition 6.4

(mild solution)
.

Consider either the setting of Proposition
5.1
and suppose that condition
(HPar)
holds. Then the map

\bar{v}:[0,T]\times\Omega\to H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})

(6.29)

defined in (
6.18
) satisfies the

H^{k}(\mathcal{D};\mathbb{R}^{n})
-valued identity
